Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. ```text
1	\section*{Limitations}
2	
3	Our controlled experiments cover three 7--8B model families with QLoRA fine-tuning; generalization to larger scales, mixture-of-experts architectures, full-parameter fine-tuning, and pretraining-stage contamination remains untested.
4	Most contamination conditions use a single training run, and multi-seed replication (available for Qwen, Llama, and Mistral) reveals high seed dependence at 50\% contamination (CV${=}$60\%), partially resolved by $\theta$-anchoring; the post-RL characterization is $N{=}1$.
5	The framework depends on pre-calibrated 4PL item parameters from PSN-IRT, whose calibration sample may include contaminated models, and we do not provide head-to-head comparison with logit-based methods (MIA, ConStat) that operate at a different information level.
6

### Tool result

_source line 2413 · 2026-05-25T14:18:19.149Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019zcsaFGLpty2UmRoYfDcze`</summary>

```text
1	Benchmark contamination in Large Language Models (LLMs) undermines evaluation validity, yet most detection methods require privileged access to training data, model internals, or output probabilities.
2	We present the first controlled characterization of Item Response Theory (IRT) person-fit statistics for detecting supervised fine-tuning (SFT) contamination, requiring only binary response patterns and calibrated four-parameter logistic item parameters; diagnostic $\theta$-anchoring requires a clean baseline.
3	A contaminated LLM produces aberrant response patterns analogous to a test-taker with item preknowledge.
4	Using $\ell_z^*$ in a matched-difference design ($\Delta\ell_z^*$) across three 7--8B model families ($n{=}10$ clean seeds per family) on MMLU (14,042 items) and ARC-Challenge (295 items), we identify three boundaries:
5	(1) model-specific $\theta$-shift dynamics, where contamination-induced ability inflation absorbs the person-fit signal; $\theta$-anchoring amplifies detection by 2.4$\times$ while reducing coefficient of variation from 60\% to 19\%;
6	(2) a detection-diagnosis tradeoff where simpler statistics yield stronger detection but lack $\ell_z^*$'s diagnostic capabilities (seen/unseen decomposition, mechanistic $\theta$-shift analysis);
7	and (3, exploratory) ability-dependent non-monotonic dose-response under free-$\theta$ estimation, driven by $\theta$-shift absorption; $\theta$-anchored analysis reveals stable positive dose-response.
8	These findings establish when and why binary response patterns carry contamination information beyond aggregate accuracy\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/irt_personfit_contam-6004/}.}.
9	
```

</details>

[飞书用户 用户]: 📐 论文自检任务：与 ACL 官方模板对齐 + 控制正文页数

请打开下面这个 ACL 官方模板，逐行、非常仔细地和我们论文目前使用的 template 对比，找出任何差异并对齐。

官方模板：
https://www.overleaf.com/latex/templates/association-for-computational-linguistics-acl-conference/jvxskxpnznfj

---

任务要求：

1. 检测任何差异
   把官方模板和我们当前论文用的 template 做完整 diff，任何差异都不能漏掉，包括但不限于：
   - 文档类参数：\documentclass[...]{acl} 的所有选项是否一致
   - preamble 中的 \usepackage：包名、版本、参数、顺序
   - 字体、字号、行距、页边距设置
   - 标题、作者、机构、邮箱区域的格式
   - Abstract 区域的环境定义
   - section / subsection / paragraph 的样式
   - 引用、参考文献的样式（natbib? bibstyle?）
   - 脚注样式
   - 表格、图片的默认样式
   - 公式编号、定理环境
   - 页眉页脚、页码
   - hyperref / url 的设置
   - 任何被我们注释掉、替换、增加的行

2. 全部向官方文档对齐
   发现任何与官方模板不一致的地方，全部改成官方模板的写法。除非有非常明确的必要原因（如 venue 要求的差异），否则不要保留任何自定义改动。如果某处差异确实有理由保留，请在汇报里单独说明原因。

3. 控制正文页数在 8 页左右
   - 正文（main text，不含 references / appendix）必须在 8 页左右
   - 严格不超过 8 页（超 1 行都不行）
   - 也不能比 8 页少太多——如果当前只有 5、6 页，说明内容不够充实，需要补充：
     * 增加实验分析 / ablation / 定性例子
     * 把过于简略的方法描述展开
     * 增加 motivation、related work、discussion 等
   - 目标是恰好填满 8 页正文

---

完成后请汇报：

(a) 我们的 template 和官方模板有哪些差异？每一处都列出来
(b) 这些差异分别是怎么修复对齐的？还是有保留的（保留的请说明原因）
(c) 修改后正文目前是几页？如果不到 8 页，准备补充哪些内容？如果超过 8 页，准备压缩哪些内容？

```text
# ACL Conference LaTeX Template - Complete Source

## Document Class and Main Preamble

The template uses `\documentclass[11pt]{article}` as its foundation. The core style package is loaded via `\usepackage[review]{acl}`, with the `review` option toggled for anonymous submissions (change to `\usepackage{acl}` for final versions, or use `preprint` for non-anonymous versions with page numbers).

## Required and Recommended Packages

**Essential packages included:**
- `times` - Times Roman font
- `latexsym` - LaTeX symbols
- `fontenc[T1]` - Proper character rendering and hyphenation
- `inputenc[utf8]` - UTF-8 file encoding support
- `microtype` - Layout and spacing improvements
- `inconsolata` - Typewriter font aesthetics
- `graphicx` - Image inclusion support

## Bibliography Configuration

The template supports natbib citation commands and includes an ACL-specific bibliography style (`acl_natbib.bst`). Bibliography files can reference `custom.bib` or combine with the ACL Anthology via: `\bibliography{anthology,custom}`. The ACL Anthology file is available as a gzip-compressed BibTeX file from the ACL web organization.

## Title and Author Format

The `\title{}` and `\author{}` commands structure metadata. Multiple authors use `\and`, `\And`, or `\AND` delimiters. The title box defaults to 5cm minimum; expand via `\setlength\titlebox{&lt;dim>}` if needed.

## Key Compilation Notes

- **Recommended engine:** pdfLaTeX (strongly preferred over dvips+ps2pdf workflows)
- **Alternative engines:** LuaLaTeX and XeLaTeX support non-Latin scripts
- The template notes that citations splitting across page boundaries previously caused PDF nesting errors in older LaTeX versions (resolved in 2018-12-01+ releases)
```

106	
107	\begin{document}
108	\maketitle
109	\begin{abstract}
110	This document is a supplement to the general instructions for *ACL authors. It contains instructions for using the \LaTeX{} style files for ACL conferences.
111	The document itself conforms to its own specifications, and is therefore an example of what your manuscript should look like.
112	These instructions should be used both for papers submitted for review and for final versions of accepted papers.
113	\end{abstract}
114	
115	\section{Introduction}
116	
117	These instructions are for authors submitting papers to *ACL conferences using \LaTeX. They are not self-contained. All authors must follow the general instructions for *ACL proceedings,\footnote{\url{http://acl-org.github.io/ACLPUB/formatting.html}} and this document contains additional instructions for the \LaTeX{} style files.
118	
119	The templates include the \LaTeX{} source of this document (\texttt{acl\_latex.tex}),
120	the \LaTeX{} style file used to format it (\texttt{acl.sty}),
121	an ACL bibliography style (\texttt{acl\_natbib.bst}),
122	an example bibliography (\texttt{custom.bib}),
123	and the bibliography for the ACL Anthology (\texttt{anthology.bib}).
124	
125	\section{Engines}
126	
127	To produce a PDF file, pdf\LaTeX{} is strongly recommended (over original \LaTeX{} plus dvips+ps2pdf or dvipdf).
128	The style file \texttt{acl.sty} can also be used with
129	lua\LaTeX{} and
130	Xe\LaTeX{}, which are especially suitable for text in non-Latin scripts.
131	The file \texttt{acl\_lualatex.tex} in this repository provides
132	an example of how to use \texttt{acl.sty} with either
133	lua\LaTeX{} or
134	Xe\LaTeX{}.
135	
136	\section{Preamble}
137	
138	The first line of the file must be
139	\begin{quote}
140	\begin{verbatim}
141	\documentclass[11pt]{article}
142	\end{verbatim}
143	\end{quote}
144	
145	To load the style file in the review version:
146	\begin{quote}
147	\begin{verbatim}
148	\usepackage[review]{acl}
149	\end{verbatim}
150	\end{quote}
151	For the final version, omit the \verb|review| option:
152	\begin{quote}
153	\begin{verbatim}
154	\usepackage{acl}
155	\end{verbatim}
156	\end{quote}
157	
158	To use Times Roman, put the following in the preamble:
159	\begin{quote}
160	\begin{verbatim}
161	\usepackage{times}
162	\end{verbatim}
163	\end{quote}
164	(Alternatives like txfonts or newtx are also acceptable.)
165	
166	Please see the \LaTeX{} source of this document for comments on other packages that may be useful.
167	
168	Set the title and author using \verb|\title| and \verb|\author|. Within the author list, format multiple authors using \verb|\and| and \verb|\And| and \verb|\AND|; please see the \LaTeX{} source for examples.
169	
170	By default, the box containing the title and author names is set to the minimum of 5 cm. If you need more space, include the following in the preamble:
171	\begin{quote}
172	\begin{verbatim}
173	\setlength\titlebox{&lt;dim>}
174	\end{verbatim}
175	\end{quote}
176	where \verb|&lt;dim>| is replaced with a length. Do not set this length smaller than 5 cm.
177	
178	\section{Document Body}
179	
180	\subsection{Footnotes}
181	
182	Footnotes are inserted with the \verb|\footnote| command.\footnote{This is a footnote.}
183	
184	\subsection{Tables and figures}
185	
186	See Table~\ref{tab:accents} for an example of a table and its caption.
187	\textbf{Do not override the default caption sizes.}
188	
189	\begin{table}
190	  \centering
191	  \begin{tabular}{lc}
192	    \hline
193	    \textbf{Command} & \textbf{Output} \\
194	    \hline
195	    \verb|{\"a}|     & {\"a}           \\
196	    \verb|{\^e}|     & {\^e}           \\
197	    \verb|{\`i}|     & {\`i}           \\
198	    \verb|{\.I}|     & {\.I}           \\
199	    \verb|{\o}|      & {\o}            \\
200	    \verb|{\'u}|     & {\'u}           \\
201	    \verb|{\aa}|     & {\aa}           \\\hline
202	  \end{tabular}
203	  \begin{tabular}{lc}
204	    \hline
205	    \textbf{Command} & \textbf{Output} \\
206	    \hline
207	    \verb|{\c c}|    & {\c c}          \\
208	    \verb|{\u g}|    & {\u g}          \\
209	    \verb|{\l}|      & {\l}            \\
210	    \verb|{\~n}|     & {\~n}           \\
211	    \verb|{\H o}|    & {\H o}          \\
212	    \verb|{\v r}|    & {\v r}          \\
213	    \verb|{\ss}|     & {\ss}           \\
214	    \hline
215	  \end{tabular}
216	  \caption{Example commands for accented characters, to be used in, \emph{e.g.}, Bib\TeX{} entries.}
217	  \label{tab:accents}
218	\end{table}
219	
220	As much as possible, fonts in figures should conform
221	to the document fonts. See Figure~\ref{fig:experiments} for an example of a figure and its caption.
222	
223	Using the \verb|graphicx| package graphics files can be included within figure
224	environment at an appropriate point within the text.
225	The \verb|graphicx| package supports various optional arguments to control the
226	appearance of the figure.
227	You must include it explicitly in the \LaTeX{} preamble (after the
228	\verb|\documentclass| declaration and before \verb|\begin{document}|) using
229	\verb|\usepackage{graphicx}|.
230	
231	\begin{figure}[t]
232	  \includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{example-image-golden}
233	  \caption{A figure with a caption that runs for more than one line.
234	    Example image is usually available through the \texttt{mwe} package
235	    without even mentioning it in the preamble.}
236	  \label{fig:experiments}
237	\end{figure}
238	
239	\begin{figure*}[t]
240	  \includegraphics[width=0.48\linewidth]{example-image-a} \hfill
241	  \includegraphics[width=0.48\linewidth]{example-image-b}
242	  \caption {A minimal working example to demonstrate how to place
243	    two images side-by-side.}
244	\end{figure*}
245	
246	\subsection{Hyperlinks}
247	
248	Users of older versions of \LaTeX{} may encounter the following error during compilation:
249	\begin{quote}
250	\verb|\pdfendlink| ended up in different nesting level than \verb|\pdfstartlink|.
251	\end{quote}
252	This happens when pdf\LaTeX{} is used and a citation splits across a page boundary. The best way to fix this is to upgrade \LaTeX{} to 2018-12-01 or later.
253	
254	\subsection{Citations}
255	
256	\begin{table*}
257	  \centering
258	  \begin{tabular}{lll}
259	    \hline
260	    \textbf{Output}           & \textbf{natbib command} & \textbf{ACL only command} \\
261	    \hline
262	    \citep{Gusfield:97}       & \verb|\citep|           &                           \\
263	    \citealp{Gusfield:97}     & \verb|\citealp|         &                           \\
264	    \citet{Gusfield:97}       & \verb|\citet|           &                           \\
265	    \citeyearpar{Gusfield:97} & \verb|\citeyearpar|     &                           \\
266	    \citeposs{Gusfield:97}    &                         & \verb|\citeposs|          \\
267	    \hline
268	  \end{tabular}
269	  \caption{\label{citation-guide}
270	    Citation commands supported by the style file.
271	    The style is based on the natbib package and supports all natbib citation commands.
272	    It also supports commands defined in previous ACL style files for compatibility.
273	  }
274	\end{table*}
275	
276	Table~\ref{citation-guide} shows the syntax supported by the style files.
277	We encourage you to use the natbib styles.
278	You can use the command \verb|\citet| (cite in text) to get ``author (year)'' citations, like this citation to a paper by \citet{Gusfield:97}.
279	You can use the command \verb|\citep| (cite in parentheses) to get ``(author, year)'' citations \citep{Gusfield:97}.
280	You can use the command \verb|\citealp| (alternative cite without parentheses) to get ``author, year'' citations, which is useful for using citations within parentheses (e.g. \citealp{Gusfield:97}).
281	
282	A possessive citation can be made with the command \verb|\citeposs|.
283	This is not a standard natbib command, so it is generally not compatible
284	with other style files.
285	
286	\subsection{References}
287	
288	\nocite{Ando2005,andrew2007scalable,rasooli-tetrault-2015}
289	
290	The \LaTeX{} and Bib\TeX{} style files provided roughly follow the American Psychological Association format.
291	If your own bib file is named \texttt{custom.bib}, then placing the following before any appendices in your \LaTeX{} file will generate the references section for you:
292	\begin{quote}
293	\begin{verbatim}
294	\bibliography{custom}
295	\end{verbatim}
296	\end{quote}
297	
298	You can obtain the complete ACL Anthology as a Bib\TeX{} file from \url{https://aclweb.org/anthology/anthology.bib.gz}.
299	To include both the Anthology and your own .bib file, use the following instead of the above.
300	\begin{quote}
301	\begin{verbatim}
302	\bibliography{anthology,custom}
303	\end{verbatim}
304	\end{quote}
305	
306	Please see Section~\ref{sec:bibtex} for information on preparing Bib\TeX{} files.
307	
308	\subsection{Equations}
309	
310	An example equation is shown below:
311	\begin{equation}
312	  \label{eq:example}
313	  A = \pi r^2
314	\end{equation}
315	
316	Labels for equation numbers, sections, subsections, figures and tables
317	are all defined with the \verb|\label{label}| command and cross references
318	to them are made with the \verb|\ref{label}| command.
319	
320	This an example cross-reference to Equation~\ref{eq:example}.
321	
322	\subsection{Appendices}
323	
324	Use \verb|\appendix| before any appendix section to switch the section numbering over to letters. See Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix} for an example.
325	
326	\section{Bib\TeX{} Files}
327	\label{sec:bibtex}
328	
329	Unicode cannot be used in Bib\TeX{} entries, and some ways of typing special characters can disrupt Bib\TeX's alphabetization. The recommended way of typing special characters is shown in Table~\ref{tab:accents}.
330	
331	Please ensure that Bib\TeX{} records contain DOIs or URLs when possible, and for all the ACL materials that you reference.
332	Use the \verb|doi| field for DOIs and the \verb|url| field for URLs.
333	If a Bib\TeX{} entry has a URL or DOI field, the paper title in the references section will appear as a hyperlink to the paper, using the hyperref \LaTeX{} package.
334	
335	\section*{Limitations}
336	
337	This document does not cover the content requirements for ACL or any
338	other specific venue.  Check the author instructions for
339	information on
340	maximum page lengths, the required ``Limitations'' section,
341	and so on.
342

362	Additional elements were taken from the formatting instructions of the \emph{International Joint Conference on Artificial Intelligence} and the \emph{Conference on Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition}.
363

366

which is useful for using citations within parentheses (e.g. \\citealp{Gusfield:97}).\n\nA possessive citation can be made with the command \\verb|\\citeposs|.\nThis is not a standard natbib command, so it is generally not compatible\nwith other style files.\n\n\\subsection{References}\n\n\\nocite{Ando2005,andrew2007scalable,rasooli-tetrault-2015}\n\nThe \\LaTeX{} and Bib\\TeX{} style files provided roughly follow the American Psychological Association format.\nIf your own bib file is named \\texttt{custom.bib}, then placing the following before any appendices in your \\LaTeX{} file will generate the references section for you:\n\\begin{quote}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n\\bibliography{custom}\n\\end{verbatim}\n\\end{quote}\n\nYou can obtain the complete ACL Anthology as a Bib\\TeX{} file from \\url{https://aclweb.org/anthology/anthology.bib.gz}.\nTo include both the Anthology and your own .bib file, use the following instead of the above.\n\\begin{quote}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n\\bibliography{anthology,custom}\n\\end{verbatim}\n\\end{quote}\n\nPlease see Section~\\ref{sec:bibtex} for information on preparing Bib\\TeX{} files.\n\n\\subsection{Equations}\n\nAn example equation is shown below:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\label{eq:example}\n  A = \\pi r^2\n\\end{equation}\n\nLabels for equation numbers, sections, subsections, figures and tables\nare all defined with the \\verb|\\label{label}| command and cross references\nto them are made with the \\verb|\\ref{label}| command.\n\nThis an example cross-reference to Equation~\\ref{eq:example}.\n\n\\subsection{Appendices}\n\nUse \\verb|\\appendix| before any appendix section to switch the section numbering over to letters. See Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix} for an example.\n\n\\section{Bib\\TeX{} Files}\n\\label{sec:bibtex}\n\nUnicode cannot be used in Bib\\TeX{} entries, and some ways of typing special characters can disrupt Bib\\TeX's alphabetization. The recommended way of typing special characters is shown in Table~\\ref{tab:accents}.\n\nPlease ensure that Bib\\TeX{} records contain DOIs or URLs when possible, and for all the ACL materials that you reference.\nUse the \\verb|doi| field for DOIs and the \\verb|url| field for URLs.\nIf a Bib\\TeX{} entry has a URL or DOI field, the paper title in the references section will appear as a hyperlink to the paper, using the hyperref \\LaTeX{} package.\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nThis document does not cover the content requirements for ACL or any\nother specific venue.  Check the author instructions for\ninformation on\nmaximum page lengths, the required ``Limitations'' section,\nand so on.\n\n\\section*{Acknowledgments}\n\nThis document has been adapted\nby Steven Bethard, Ryan Cotterell and Rui Yan\nfrom the instructions for earlier ACL and NAACL proceedings, including those for\nACL 2019 by Douwe Kiela and Ivan Vuli\\'{c},\nNAACL 2019 by Stephanie Lukin and Alla Roskovskaya,\nACL 2018 by Shay Cohen, Kevin Gimpel, and Wei Lu,\nNAACL 2018 by Margaret Mitchell and Stephanie Lukin,\nBib\\TeX{} suggestions for (NA)ACL 2017/2018 from Jason Eisner,\nACL 2017 by Dan Gildea and Min-Yen Kan,\nNAACL 2017 by Margaret Mitchell,\nACL 2012 by Maggie Li and Michael White,\nACL 2010 by Jing-Shin Chang and Philipp Koehn,\nACL 2008 by Johanna D. The only real template-level discrepancy is the missing `inconsolata` package for the typewriter font, so I'll add that back in. After that, I need to recompile and verify the document still fits within the 8-page limit, then summarize all the differences found and how they were resolved.

1	"""
2	Figure 1 (composite): 2×2 panel figure for EMNLP 2026.

6	Designed to publication-level quality: NPG palette, serif fonts, subtle details.

9	import matplotlib
10	matplotlib.use('Agg')
11	
12	import sys, shutil
13	import numpy as np
14	import pandas as pd
15	import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
16	import matplotlib.patheffects as pe

18	from matplotlib.gridspec import GridSpec
19	from matplotlib.patches import FancyBboxPatch
20	from pathlib import Path
21	from scipy.ndimage import uniform_filter1d
22	
23	sys.path.insert(0, '/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0519-3/projects/irt_personfit_contam/figures/paper')
24	import palette
25	palette.apply()
26	
27	PROJ = Path('/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0519-3/projects/irt_personfit_contam')
28

30	
31	# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
32	# DATA: panels (a)+(b) — IRT response patterns
33	# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
34	np.random.seed(42)

42	b_param = items['b_difficulty'].values
43	c_param = items['c_guessing'].values
44	d_param = items['d_feasibility'].values
45	
46	theta = 0.5
47	p_irt = c_param + (d_param - c_param) / (1 + np.exp(-a_param * (theta - b_param)))
48	
49	clean = (np.random.random(N) < p_irt).astype(int)
50	
51	contam = clean.copy()
52	hard_wrong_idx = np.where((b_param > 0.3) & (contam == 0))[0]
53	np.random.seed(42)
54	n_flip = min(45, len(hard_wrong_idx))
55	flip_hard = np.random.choice(hard_wrong_idx, n_flip, replace=False)
56	contam[flip_hard] = 1
57	
58	memorized = np.zeros(N, dtype=bool)
59	memorized[flip_hard] = True
60	
61	def lz_star(resp, a, b, c, d, th):
62	    pr = np.clip(c + (d - c) / (1 + np.exp(-a * (th - b))), 1e-10, 1 - 1e-10)
63	    ll = np.sum(resp * np.log(pr) + (1 - resp) * np.log(1 - pr))
64	    mu = np.sum(pr * np.log(pr) + (1 - pr) * np.log(1 - pr))
65	    var = np.sum(pr * (1 - pr) * (np.log(pr / (1 - pr)))**2)
66	    return (ll - mu) / np.sqrt(var)
67	
68	lz_c = lz_star(clean, a_param, b_param, c_param, d_param, theta)
69	lz_t = lz_star(contam, a_param, b_param, c_param, d_param, theta)
70	delta_lz = abs(lz_c - lz_t)
71	print(f"ℓz* clean={lz_c:.2f}, contam={lz_t:.2f}, |Δℓz*|={delta_lz:.1f}")
72	
73	p_smooth = uniform_filter1d(p_irt, size=7, mode='nearest')
74	x_items = np.arange(N)
75

89	# HELPER: rug ticks
90	# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
91	def draw_rug_ticks(ax, positions, y_centers, color, alpha=0.55, lw=0.9, height=0.05):
92	    y_centers = np.broadcast_to(np.asarray(y_centers, dtype=float), positions.shape)
93	    segments = [[(float(xi), float(yc - height / 2)),
94	                 (float(xi), float(yc + height / 2))]
95	                for xi, yc in zip(positions, y_centers)]
96	    if not segments:
97	        return
98	    lc = LineCollection(segments, colors=color, linewidths=lw, alpha=alpha, zorder=4)
99	    ax.add_collection(lc)
100	
101	# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
102	# PANELS (a) + (b): response patterns
103	# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
104	def plot_response_panel(ax, responses, panel_label, subtitle, is_contam=False):
105	    # Gradient fill under IRT curve (layered for smooth effect)
106	    for i in range(15):
107	        frac_lo = i / 15
108	        frac_hi = (i + 1) / 15
109	        alpha = 0.07 * (1.0 - frac_lo * 0.9)
110	        ax.fill_between(x_items, p_smooth * frac_lo, p_smooth * frac_hi,
111	                        alpha=alpha, color=palette.CURVE, linewidth=0, zorder=1)
112	
113	    # IRT probability curve
114	    ax.plot(x_items, p_smooth, color=palette.CURVE, linewidth=1.8, alpha=0.8, zorder=3)
115	
116	    # Response rug ticks
117	    correct = responses == 1
118	    wrong = responses == 0
119	    np.random.seed(77)
120	    jitter_1 = np.random.uniform(-0.01, 0.01, N)
121	    jitter_0 = np.random.uniform(-0.01, 0.01, N)
122	
123	    if is_contam:
124	        norm_c = correct & ~memorized
125	        draw_rug_ticks(ax, x_items[norm_c], 1.0 + jitter_1[norm_c],
126	                       palette.CURVE, alpha=0.50, lw=0.9)
127	        draw_rug_ticks(ax, x_items[wrong], 0.0 + jitter_0[wrong],
128	                       '#CCCCCC', alpha=0.55, lw=0.9)
129	
130	        # Memorized items: prominent red diamonds
131	        mem_x = x_items[memorized]
132	        mem_y = np.ones(memorized.sum()) + jitter_1[memorized]
133	        ax.scatter(mem_x, mem_y,
134	                   s=35, color=palette.SIGNAL, alpha=0.92, edgecolors='white',
135	                   linewidths=0.7, zorder=7, marker='D')
136	
137	        # Connection lines from memorized items to IRT curve
138	        for idx in flip_hard:
139	            ax.plot([x_items[idx], x_items[idx]],

141	                    color=palette.SIGNAL, alpha=0.18, linewidth=0.5,

143

Dose-Response Trajectory\n\nDesigned to publication-level quality: NPG palette, serif fonts, subtle details.\nOutput: fig_1_composite.{pdf,png}\n\"\"\"\nimport matplotlib\nmatplotlib.use('Agg')\n\nimport sys, shutil\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nimport matplotlib.patheffects as pe\nfrom matplotlib.collections import LineCollection\nfrom matplotlib.gridspec import GridSpec\nfrom matplotlib.patches import FancyBboxPatch\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom scipy.ndimage import uniform_filter1d\n\nsys.path.insert(0, '/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0519-3/projects/irt_personfit_contam/figures/paper')\nimport palette\npalette.apply()\n\nPROJ = ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n# DATA: panels (a)+(b) — IRT response patterns\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\nnp.random.seed(42)\ndf = pd.read_csv(PROJ / 'artifacts' / 'psn_irt_params' / 'mmlu_params.csv')\nN = 300\ndf_sorted = df.sort_values('b_difficulty')\nindices = np.linspace(0, len(df) - 1, N, dtype=int)\nitems = df_sorted.iloc[indices].reset_index(drop=True).sort_values('b_difficulty').reset_index(drop=True)\n\na_param = items['a_discrimination'].values\nb_param = items['b_difficulty'].values\nc_param = items['c_guessing'].values\nd_param = items['d_feasibility'].values\n\ntheta = 0.5\np_irt = c_param + (d_param - c_param) / (1 + np.exp(-a_param * (theta - b_param)))\n\nclean = (np.random.random(N) < p_irt).astype(int)\n\ncontam = clean.copy()\nhard_wrong_idx = np.where((b_param > 0.3) & (contam == 0))[0]\nnp.random.seed(42)\nn_flip = min(45, len(hard_wrong_idx))\nflip_hard = np.random.choice(hard_wrong_idx, n_flip, replace=False)\ncontam[flip_hard] = 1\n\nmemorized = np.zeros(N, dtype=bool)\nmemorized[flip_hard] = True\n\ndef lz_star(resp, a, b, c, d, th):\n    pr = np.clip(c + (d - c) / (1 + np.exp(-a * (th - b))), 1e-10, 1 - 1e-10)\n    ll = np.sum(resp * np.log(pr) + (1 - resp) * np.log(1 - pr))\n    mu = np.sum(pr * np.log(pr) + (1 - pr) * np.log(1 - pr))\n    var = np.sum(pr * (1 - pr) * (np.log(pr / (1 - pr)))**2)\n    return (ll - mu) / np.sqrt(var)\n\nlz_c = lz_star(clean, a_param, b_param, c_param, d_param, theta)\nlz_t = lz_star(contam, a_param, b_param, c_param, d_param, theta)\ndelta_lz = abs(lz_c - lz_t)\nprint(f\"ℓz* clean={lz_c:.2f}, contam={lz_t:.2f}, |Δℓz*|={delta_lz:.1f}\")\n\np_smooth = uniform_filter1d(p_irt, size=7, mode='nearest')\nx_items = np.arange(N)\n\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n# FIGURE LAYOUT\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\nfig = plt.figure(figsize=(7.2, 5.8))\ngs = GridSpec(2, 2, figure=fig, wspace=0.30, hspace=0.48,\n              left=0.08, right=0.97, top=0.96, bottom=0.08)\n\nax_a = fig.add_subplot(gs[0, 0])\nax_b = fig.add_subplot(gs[0, 1], sharey=ax_a)\nax_c = fig.add_subplot(gs[1, 0])\nax_d = fig.add_subplot(gs[1, 1])\n\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n# HELPER: rug ticks\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\ndef draw_rug_ticks(ax, positions, y_centers, color, alpha=0.55, lw=0.9, height=0.05):\n    y_centers = np.broadcast_to(np.asarray(y_centers, dtype=float), positions.shape)\n    segments = [[(float(xi), float(yc - height / 2)),\n                 (float(xi), float(yc + height / 2))]\n                for xi, yc in zip(positions, y_centers)]\n    if not segments:\n        return\n    lc = LineCollection(segments, colors=color, linewidths=lw, alpha=alpha, zorder=4)\n    ax.add_collection(lc)\n\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n# PANELS (a) + (b): response patterns\n# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\ndef plot_response_panel(ax, responses, panel_label, subtitle, is_contam=False):\n    # Gradient fill under IRT curve (layered for smooth effect)\n    for i in range(15):\n        frac_lo = i / 15\n        frac_hi = (i + 1) / 15\n        alpha = 0.07 * (1.0 - frac_lo * 0.9)\n        ax.fill_between(x_items, p_smooth * frac_lo, p_smooth * frac_hi,\n                        alpha=alpha, color=palette.CURVE, linewidth=0, zorder=1)\n\n    # IRT probability curve\n    ax.plot(x_items, p_smooth, color=palette.CURVE, linewidth=1.8, alpha=0.8, zorder=3)\n\n    # Response rug ticks\n    correct = responses == 1\n    wrong = responses == 0\n    np.random.seed(77)\n    jitter_1 = np.random.uniform(-0.01, 0.01, N)\n    jitter_0 = np.random.uniform(-0.01, 0.01, N)\n\n    if is_contam:\n        norm_c = correct & ~memorized\n        draw_rug_ticks(ax, x_items[norm_c], 1.0 + jitter_1[norm_c],\n                       palette.CURVE, alpha=0.50, lw=0.9)\n        draw_rug_ticks(ax, x_items[wrong], 0.0 + jitter_0[wrong],\n                       '#CCCCCC', alpha=0.55, lw=0.9)\n\n        # Memorized items: prominent red diamonds\n        mem_x = x_items[memorized]\n        mem_y = np.ones(memorized.sum()) + jitter_1[memorized]\n        ax.scatter(mem_x, mem_y,\n                   s=35, color=palette.SIGNAL, alpha=0.92, edgecolors='white',\n                   linewidths=0.7, zorder=7, marker='D')\n\n        # Connection lines from memorized items to IRT curve\n        for idx in flip_hard:\n            ax.plot([x_items[idx], x_items[idx]],\n                    [p_smooth[idx], 1.0 + jitter_1[idx]],\n                    color=palette.SIGNAL, alpha=0.18, linewidth=0.5,\n                    linestyle=(0, (3, 3)), zorder=2)\n\n        # Small legend for memorized items\n        ax.scatter([], [], s=25, color=palette.SIGNAL, marker='D',\n                   edgecolors='white', linewidths=0.5, label='Memorized (seen)')\n        leg = ax.legend(loc='lower right', frameon=True, framealpha=0.95,\n                        edgecolor='#E0E0E0', fontsize=6, handletextpad=0.3,\n                        borderpad=0.4, bbox_to_anchor=(0.98, 0.15))\n        leg.get_frame().set_linewidth(0.3)\n    else:\n        draw_rug_ticks(ax, x_items[correct], 1.0 + jitter_1[correct],\n                       palette.CURVE, alpha=0.50, lw=0.9)\n        draw_rug_ticks(ax, x_items[wrong], 0.0 + jitter_0[wrong],\n                       '#CCCCCC', alpha=0.55, lw=0.9)\n\n    # Faint horizontal guides at 0 and 1\n    ax.axhline(y=0, color='#000000', alpha=0.05, linewidth=0.4, zorder=0)\n    ax.axhline(y=1, color='#000000', alpha=0.05, linewidth=0.4, zorder=0)\n\n    # Panel label\n    ax.text(0.02, 0.97, panel_label, transform=ax.transAxes,\n            fontsize=11, fontweight='bold', va='top', ha='left',\n            color=palette.TEXT, zorder=10)\n    # Subtitle — positioned above the y=1 rug marks\n    ax.text(0.14, 0.97, subtitle, transform=ax.transAxes,\n            fontsize=7.5, va='top', ha='left',\n            color=palette.ANNOTATION, fontstyle='italic', zorder=10)\n\n    # ℓz* badge box\n    if is_contam:\n        lz_color = palette.SIGNAL\n        bg_color = '#FDF0EE'\n        lz_text = f'$|\\\\Delta\\\\ell_z^*|$ = {delta_lz:.1f}'\n    else:\n        lz_color = '#27AE60'\n        bg_color = '#F0F7F0'\n        lz_text = f'$\\\\ell_z^*$ = {lz_c:+.1f}'\n\n    ax.text(0.50, -0.22, [飞书用户 用户]: 📊 论文自检任务（重大）：全文数据真实性与可追溯性彻底审计

这是一次彻底的大检查，必须认真对待。AI 写论文最致命的两个问题：编数字（hallucinate）和抄数字（数字之间不一致或来源不明）。这次的目标是把每一个出现在论文里的数字都"对账"清楚。

⚠️ 凡是查不到出处的数字，要么找到出处，要么删掉。不允许凭印象、凭"差不多"、凭老版本残留的数字留在论文里。

---

审计范围（无遗漏）：

1. 图（Figures）
   - 每张图里的数据点、bar 高度、曲线值、error bar
   - 图的 caption 里出现的数字（"reduces by 23%"、"on average 4.2"）
   - 坐标轴 tick 标注

2. 表（Tables）
   - 主结果表、ablation 表、超参表、基线表
   - 单元格里的每个数字，包括精度（小数位数）
   - 表头里的尺寸说明（"N=1000"、"k=5"）

3. 正文（Body）
   - Abstract 里的关键 claim 数字
   - Introduction 的 contribution 数字
   - Method 描述里的超参（learning rate、batch、steps、模型尺寸、参数量）
   - Experimental setup（数据集大小、训练 GPU 小时数、wall-clock 时间）
   - Analysis 段落里逐一引用的数字
   - Conclusion 里复述的数字

4. 附录（Appendix）
   - 完整的超参表、额外实验结果
   - 数据集详细统计
   - 失败案例的统计
   - Proof 里的常数、bound

---

对每个数字，逐个确认这 3 个问题：

Q1（真实性）：这个数字是从一次 真实运行 的实验里出来的吗？

合格证据：
- 在 results.yaml / 实验日志 / WandB run 里能定位到原始记录
- registry 里有对应的实验 ID
- 知道是 seed N 跑的、还是 mean of N seeds

不合格：
❌ "之前跑过一版差不多是这个数"
❌ "我们估算的"（除非论文明确说明 "estimated"）
❌ "上次 AI 写的时候放进来的，我也不知道哪来的"
❌ "占位符忘删了"

Q2（正确性）：抄到论文里有没有抄错？

- 原始记录 73.18% 抄到表里有没有变成 73.81% 或 73.2%？
- 小数位精度跟原始一致吗？（不要四舍五入到误差范围以外）
- 单位对吗（accuracy vs F1，pp vs %，BLEU vs ROUGE）

Q3（可追溯性）：数字写在论文里几个地方，每个地方都一致吗？

同一个指标可能出现在：Abstract、Introduction contribution、Method、Main Table、Analysis 段落、Conclusion —— 必须逐字逐位一致。不允许 Abstract 说 73.2%、表里 73.18%、Conclusion 又变成 73%。

---

审计步骤（请按这个顺序）：

1. 列清单：把论文里出现的所有数字（图+表+正文+附录）列出来。Director 自己估算一下总共多少个数字（一般 200-500 个）。

2. 逐个对账：每个数字找 results.yaml / wandb / 日志 / registry 里的源头。能找到就在自检表里记一行 "数字 X → 来源 Y"。

3. 不一致清单：列出 (a) 找不到出处的，(b) 跟源头对不上的，(c) 论文内部前后不一致的。

4. 决策：每个不一致项决定怎么修：
   - 能找到出处 → 改成正确的，并保证全文一致
   - 实在找不到出处 → 删掉，不要硬撑

5. 二次扫描：修完后再过一遍正文，确认没有遗留。

---

汇报内容（完成后）：

(a) 总共审计了多少个数字？
(b) 发现多少个"不一致 / 找不到出处"的问题？分别在哪些章节？
(c) 这些问题分别是怎么处理的（修了 / 删了）？
(d) 修完后，论文里还有哪些数字是"暂时找不到出处但保留"的？为什么？

---

这次检查不是走过场。reviewer 拿到论文后只要随便挑一个数字让你说出处，你都得说得出来。请认真做，做完汇报。

## Summary of Experimental Result Data Files and Analysis Scripts

I've found a comprehensive collection of result data files, analysis scripts, and the experiment registry for the IRT person-fit contamination detection project. Here's the complete inventory:

### 1. **EXPERIMENT REGISTRY** (Central Repository of All Experiments)

**File:** `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0519-3/projects/irt_personfit_contam/registry.yaml`

Contains metadata for all experiments with detailed metrics including:
- `sim_001`: Null check and simulation (conclusion: negative)
- `phase_b_personfit`: Phase B person-fit analysis on 12 models across 5 benchmarks
- `exp-two-sided`: Two-sided detection framework (95% flagging rate)
- `exp-extended-sim`: Extended simulation across 11 contamination levels (0%-100%)
- `exp-empirical-null`: Bootstrap empirical null analysis
- `exp-power-theory`: Theoretical power analysis with R²=0.957
- `exp-leaderboard-data-recon`: Data availability survey
- `exp-item-alignment-check`: Item alignment validation across v1 leaderboard

### 2. **EXPERIMENTAL PLAN**

**File:** `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0519-3/projects/irt_personfit_contam/experiment_plan.yaml`

Defines all planned experiments with priorities, GPU budgets, and dependencies.

### 3. **PRIMARY RESULT DATA FILES** (In `artifacts/_project/data/`)

#### Matrix and Summary Data:
- `/artifacts/_project/data/personfit_matrix.csv` - Core person-fit results across 12 models × 5 benchmarks
- `/artifacts/_project/data/personfit_matrix_v2.csv` - Revised version with two-sided detection
- `/artifacts/_project/data/subject_lz_matrix.csv` - Per-subject lz* values for MMLU domain analysis
- `/artifacts/_project/data/two_sided_summary_v2.json` - Two-sided detection metrics (flagging rates, p-values)
- `/artifacts/_project/data/two_sided_power.csv` - Two-sided power curves across contamination levels

#### Delta (Contamination) Analysis:
- `/artifacts/_project/data/delta_lz_results.csv` - Δlz* between clean and contaminated conditions with t-tests, Cohen's d
- `/artifacts/_project/data/delta_summary.json` - Summary of contamination effects

#### Statistical Results:
- `/artifacts/_project/data/empirical_pvalues.csv` - Bootstrap empirical p-values for flagging
- `/artifacts/_project/data/seed_sensitivity_analysis.json` - Multi-seed results for Qwen/Llama models (mean, std, CI for lz*, accuracy, theta)
- `/artifacts/_project/data/ht_zscore_results_n10.json` - Hypothesis test results with z-scores
- `/artifacts/_project/data/qwen_theta_fixed_consistent.json` - Qwen theta estimates across seeds

#### Theory and Simulation:
- `/artifacts/_project/data/dual_regime_summary.json` - Summary of dual-regime behavior (where lz* switches sign)
- `/artifacts/_project/data/testlet_sim_results.json` - Simulated testlet effects
- `/artifacts/_project/data/param_perturbation_results.json` - Sensitivity analysis of IRT parameter perturbations
- `/artifacts/_project/data/appendix_4pl_ablation.csv` - 4PL vs 3PL ablation study results

#### Reporting:
- `/artifacts/_project/report/personfit_summary.json` - Final summary for paper

### 4. **PHASE-SPECIFIC RESULTS**

- `/artifacts/phase_b_results/` - Phase B analysis (12 PSN-IRT models)
  - `personfit_summary.json` - Key metrics: lz* distributions, Gemini-1.5 anomalies
  - `personfit_matrix.csv`, `two_sided_flags.csv`
  
- `/artifacts/phase_b_two_sided_v2/` - Two-sided framework output
- `/artifacts/extended_simulation/` - Extended simulation results (contamination levels 0%-100%)
- `/artifacts/empirical_null_analysis/` - Bootstrap null calibration

### 5. **CONTAMINATION CONTROL EXPERIMENTS**

- `/artifacts/exp-ctrl-contam-llama-25pct/data/llama_contam_25_cohens_d.json`
  - Contains: `lz_star`, `theta_mle`, `accuracy_overall`, `accuracy_seen/unseen`
  - Cohen's d: **-2.9667** with 95% CI [-6.06, -2.03]
  - Dose-response pattern documented

### 6. **PYTHON ANALYSIS SCRIPTS FOR COHEN'S D & EFFECT SIZES**

These compute final numbers reported in the paper:

#### Llama Model Analysis:
- `/paper/llama/compute_cohens_d.py` - Computes Cohen's d with bootstrap percentile + BCa CIs
- `/paper/llama/eval_lz.py` - Evaluates fine-tuned model, computes lz* statistic
- `/paper/llama/theta_fixed_recompute.py` - Recomputes theta under fixed calibration

#### Qwen Model Analysis:
- `/paper/qwen/compute_cohens_d.py` - Cohen's d computation for contam_5 with 10 clean seeds
- `/paper/qwen/compute_contam5_cohens_d.py` - Specialized contam_5 analysis
- `/paper/qwen/eval_lz.py` - Evaluates lz* across conditions
- `/paper/qwen/theta_fixed_lzstar.py` - Fixed-theta lz* recomputation
- `/paper/qwen/unified_lz_recompute.py` - Unified lz* computation across seeds

#### Mistral Model Analysis:
- `/paper/mistral/eval_lz.py` - Mistral evaluation
- `/paper/mistral/theta_fixed_n10_recompute.py` - n=10 theta recomputation

#### Data Preparation:
- `/paper/qwen/prepare_data.py` - Prepares dataset with contamination
- `/paper/qwen/gen_contam_5.py`, `gen_contam_5_s2.py`, `gen_contam_5_s3.py` - Multi-seed contamination generation

### 7. **SIMULATION & THEORY SCRIPTS**

- `/figures/paper/gen_fig3.py` - Generates contamination dose-response figures
- `/figures/paper/gen_fig_heatmap.py` - Generates lz* heatmaps across models/benchmarks
- `/artifacts/baseline_comparison.py` - Compares against 5+ baselines

### 8. **KEY NUMERICAL RESULTS FOUND**

From `delta_lz_results.csv`:
- **MMLU**: Δlz* = 9.42 (clean vs contaminated), Cohen's d = **-2.66**
- **HellaSwag**: Δlz* = 5.05, Cohen's d = **-1.85**
- **GSM8K**: Δlz* = 3.30, Cohen's d = **-1.85**
- **Arc-C**: Δlz* = 0.65, Cohen's d = **-0.45**
- **HumanEval**: Δlz* = 1.41, Cohen's d = **-1.07**

Per-seed lz* values (from `seed_sensitivity_analysis.json`):
- **Qwen2.5-7B**: mean lz* = 8.68, std = 1.41, CV = 16.3%
- **Llama-3.1-8B**: mean lz* = -0.14, std = 0.97, CV = 686%

### 9. **WORKER LOGS WITH RESULTS**

Result JSON files from worker execution logs (in `/logs/workers/`):
- `worker_code_bootstrap-cohens-d_*_result.json`
- `worker_ops_fixed-theta-cohens-d_*_result.json`
- `worker_analysis_mistral_contam_25_*_result.json`
- `worker_code_arcc-bootstrap-ci_*_result.json`
- `worker_ops_bootstrap-ci-llama_*_result.json`

All of these contain detailed test execution results and metrics.

64	\paragraph{Fixed-$\theta$ estimation.}
65	Table~\ref{tab:theta_fixed} compares free- and fixed-$\theta$ Cohen's $d$.
66	The amplification ratio tracks baseline $|\ell_z^*|$ monotonically: Qwen ($\ell_z^*{=}{+}8.68$) shows $2.4{\times}$ at 50\%, Mistral ($+4.02$) $1.7{\times}$, while Llama ($+0.26$) shows $0.4{\times}$ despite comparable $\theta$-shift ($\Delta\theta{=}0.52$).
67	Fixed-$\theta$ is thus a targeted remedy for models with substantial baseline misfit, not a universal one.
68	
69	\begin{table}[t]
70	\centering

For clean seeds:

- Clean models have no seen items since they weren't trained on any benchmark data, so the decomposition only applies to contaminated models. The paper's Δ values for seen items at 15% contamination (Δ=-14.2) and unseen items (Δ=-1.8) must be comparing contaminated seen/unseen performance against some clean baseline, though it's unclear whether that's the mean across clean models or a specific reference point.

- I'm calculating what the deltas should be if I use the clean baseline mean of 8.684, but the numbers don't match the paper's reported values at all. The contaminated model's overall lz* is 3.849, giving a delta of -4.835 against the clean mean, which still doesn't align with the individual seen and unseen deltas the paper reports.

- I'm wondering if these -14.2 and -1.8 values come from a different seed or a different computation method entirely, like using fixed-θ lz* values instead. The fixed-θ approach would produce different lz* values, though that would typically make contaminated items' values larger, not more negative.

Actually, I think I've been misinterpreting what Δ means. It might be the shift in lz* computed separately within each subset—so I'd calculate lz*_contam for just the seen items and compare it to lz*_clean across all items, then do the same for unseen items. With seed 42, that gives me Δ_seen ≈ -8.01 and Δ_unseen ≈ -4.77, which still doesn't match -14.2 or -1.8.

Or maybe the clean model's lz* needs to be computed on the same seen/unseen subsets rather than the full dataset, even though the clean model wasn't trained on those items. That would explain why the values differ, but the -14.2 shift seems implausibly large, and I can't verify this without actually running the computation.
